<div class="bsky-card" markdown="1">

<div class="bsky-header" markdown="1">

</div>

<div class="bsky-content" markdown="1">

atproto ui ux Dear UX & ATproto knowers, how should @colibri.social present sign-up opportunities to new users? I'm not talking people who know ATproto, I'm talking complete newbies. Here's the current ones, consensus on stream was they're too technical. How might that flow work?



<div class="bsky-image-grid bsky-grid-3" markdown="1">



![The login screen for colibri.social.  Step 1 of 3  What is your handle?  Sign in with the ATmosphere account you already have. Colibri never sees your password.  [Handle Input]  [Continue Button]  New to the ATmosphere? [Create an account hyperlink]](https://cdn.bsky.app/img/feed_fullsize/plain/did:plc:w64dlsa4zwjv2wljlvmymldc/bafkreidprnc3jnv5ckireccl3sbtmo76yw434mo2ufbjo5c7q7fsbxxfoy){ .bsky-img }

On Bluesky, this means that your posts will only appear in the Discover feed to people who already follow you.  Enabling this preference publishes it as a Lexicon record on your account:  {   &#34;$type&#34;: &#34;app.bsky.actor.contentVisibilityDeclaration&#34;,   &#34;hideFromAlgorithmicRecommendations&#34;: true }  (my highlight) This means that the preference can be honored by any other feed. It is up to the discretion of other feed providers to determine whether they should respect it. In general, we do not expect that topical feeds should respect this preference — even if some topical feeds make use of algorithmic sorting, they are generally opt-in in the first place. We do recommend that general non-chronological feeds, including in other apps, follow this preference. <div class="bsky-content" markdown="1">

Still think we should deprecate MCP. It was built around the idea of tool calls, which turned out to be a mistake IMHO. And codemode is a limited hack. Models mostly output Bash scripts anyway and they should probably *only* output Bash scripts. Structured output can be produced from bash scripts.
